When the emperor heard these words, as he was just and virtuous, he admired her intelligence and her wisdom and, as he feared God and dreaded him, he ordered on the spot to take her back with the servants, the eunuchs, and the soldiers to her convent. The superior and the nuns came joyfully to meet her, thanking Our Lord the Messiah who had saved her and brought her back without stain. When the nuns questioned her, she informed them of everything that had happened and told it to them. They praised God who protects those who trust in him. As for the enemy, the hateful adversary, he did not cease his struggle against the blessed Bartânoubâ. He went in haste to the land of the Persians (El-Fors) where the idolaters live and spread the fame of this virgin. It reached the king who was an idolater, did not know God, and loved guilty pleasures. He sent a troop of soldiers and eunuchs and wrote to the emperor Constantine perfidious letters, fearing that he might move his troops from Rome, and sent them with his soldiers to the emperor Constantine. He said to them: "Go to the land of Rome and ask for the convent where Bartânoubâ is; when you have found her, seize her and bring her to me quickly; hasten to cross this distance by traveling day and night: if you bring her to me,


[p. 658] {#po.11.p658}
واسألوا عن دير العذارى الذى فيه برتانوبا واذا وجدتموها اخطفوها واتونى بها سريعا واقطعوا المدة بالسفر فى الليل والنهار واذا ما انتم اوصلتموها اعطيتكم الكرامات العخليمة فخرجوا ونازوا بقلق من اجل الروم وصادفوا الدير الذى فيه العذرى فألوا عنها كانهم يريدوا يتاركوا منها ولما وجدوها اندهشوا لحسنبا وتحققوا انها المطلوة فحملوها للوقت بقلق عظيم ووقفوا با الى الملك وكانت متفكرة ماذا اصنع فى هذا القتال الردى الذى هو اشر من الاول ذهبوا بى الى ملك يخاف الله ولا يصنع بى شرا فاما هذد الدعة نهذا رجل وثنى لا يعرف الله ولعلى اجد فرصة فاستشهد بالحقيقة ولما احضروها ومثلوها امام فتطلع اليها ورمقها بشهوة عظيمة ردية وتعجب من حسنبا اما هى فلم تنظر اليه ولا تامات زخرفة بلاطه بل كان عقلبا فى السموات عند السيد المسيح اما الملك فامن صقاليته فزينوا مجالسه وادخلوا اليه العذراء الى الخدر اما العذراء فكانت مطرقة الوجه ولم ترى ان ترقعه الى فوق فقال ليا انتى برتانونا الى وصل الى خبر حسنها وصيتها وصرت لا اقدر انام لاجلبا واليوم انا قد ظفرت بمطلوى وهوذا انا أكتب لك ناثين مدينة تسودين عليه واسلم فى يديك مفاتيح خزائن اموالى لتملكى ارش فارس وحجارتها الكريمة وجواهرها الثمينة
I will grant you very great honors." They left troubled because of the Romans and arrived at the convent where the virgin was. They asked for her as if they wanted to receive her blessing. When they found her, they were stupefied by her beauty and recognized that it was she who was desired. They took her away on the spot with a great tumult and brought her to the king. She reflected: "What to do in this terrible struggle, worse than the first, for I was led to a king who feared God and did not do me harm. This time, it is a pagan who does not know God. Perhaps I will find an opportunity and will suffer martyrdom for the truth." When they brought her and made her appear before him, he looked at her and examined her with a vivid and guilty passion and marveled at her beauty. As for her, she did not look at him and did not cast a glance at the gilding of the palace, but her spirit was in the heavens, near Our Lord the Messiah. The king ordered the eunuchs to decorate the halls and to have her enter the private chamber: the saint lowered her head and did not raise it. - The king said to her: "It is you, Bartânoubâ, whose reputation for beauty has come to me; I could not sleep because of it and today I have realized my desire; I will assign you thirty cities of which you will be the mistress; I will place in your hands the keys of the treasures of my wealth, so that you will possess the land of Persia, its precious stones, its jewels of price; you will be my


[p. 659] {#po.11.p659}
وتصيرى لى» امرأة حرة وتعبد لك سائر صقالبتى وخصاياى ونكون تحت سلطانك وطاعتك اما هى فقالت له ان كنت قد اعجتك وصلحت لك انا عدتك فانا فى يديك واجتنى الى هذا المقدار فانى بذلك فرحة مسرورة بل انى قد تعمت جدا فى الطريق من السفر الصعب ؤثيابى قد اتسبخت وعيد البى اغدا وانا احتاج الى ملبوس وبخود وطيب ويا افل كى اكون نقية نظيفة1 كحسب كرامتك وقد احتاج الى حطب ومكان متعزل مفرود فى عزلة لا يكون فيها احد لارفع قريانا لالاهى قبل دخولى اليك ولكيلا اشاهد احد قريانى واريد ان تقضى غرضى فى مسثلة اخرى كن تكمل جميع مسرتى ان كنت انا اعجتك فقال لها بشهوة العدو وهو فرحان مسرور ساقضى جميع ما تجيبين بفرح قلت له قد خطر ببالى وتيقظت حواسى انى ساموت لك وهذا هو فرحى وعزى واريد تقسم لى بإيمان مصوداتك المعظمة ان اليوم الذى اموت فيه تامر بحمل رمتى الى كورتى وتسلموها لخواتى ص يقمرونى فى مقيرة اباى هذا هو التفضل والاكرام المتناهى الذى تفعله معى وتكمل صنيعه لى فنبض الملك بفرح واقسم ليا بالبته ومعبوداته انه يكمل جميع
wife of free condition, all my eunuchs will obey you and will be under your power and your authority." She replied to him: "If I please you and if I suit you, I am your servant and I am in your power; you love me to this degree, I am joyful and content; but I have been very tired on the road by this arduous journey; my clothes are soiled and the feast of my God arrives tomorrow; I need clothes, perfumes, scents, and what is necessary to wash myself in order to be clean and cleansed as is fitting for your munificence: I need wood and an isolated and solitary place in a dwelling where there is no one so that I may offer a sacrifice to my God before entering to you and without anyone seeing my offering. I desire that you fulfill my desire in another matter so that my joy may be complete if I please you." The enemy said to her with passion, for he was joyful and content: "I will execute with joy everything that you ask." She continued: "It has come to my mind, and I have the premonition that I will die before you: it is for me a joy and a consolation. I want you to swear to me by your faith in your great divinities that the day I die, you will have my bones transported to my country and that you will return them to my sisters so that they may bury me in the cemetery of my fathers: that is the extreme grace and favor that you will do for me and your benefit will be complete." The king rose with joy and swore to her by his gods and his divinities that he would fulfill all her requests. He left her and gave


[p. 660] {#po.11.p660}
مطلوباتى وخرج من عندها وولم وليمة لعظمائه وقبادمته وسائر احناده ثم عمد الى ماء ودلوك ودخور واطياب حسنة وادخليم اليها وجعل الاحطاب فى موضع مغزل ليس فيه احد وامسر ان يوقد تحتها النار فقامت وغسات وحها وايديها واقدامها وتركت تراجا من داخل على جسدها ولبست ثونا ابيضا برانى ووقفت وصلت ورفعت بخورها وذهبت الى حيث النار وقفلت الباب الى ان اشتعات النار وتضرعت وطابت من الرب الميد يسوع المسيح وقاات يا سيدى انت مت عنى ونا ايضا اموت عنك فى هذه الساعة ورشمك فى وحبى مختوما بصورتى وصرت لك كنة وعروسة اسألك يا سيدى اقبل قرانى من يدى بالعاجل اقبل نفسى لاسجد لوالدتك البتول العذراء وانا بخاتم عذرتى والقت جسدها فى النار واسلمت روجا فالتصقت ثياها يجسدها ولم تحترق لم تدنو النار من جسدها وشعن رأسا لم تحترق منه شعرة واحدة ولما تطول الامن ولم تخرج فتحوا الصقالبة الباب والخصايا ورأوها راقدة فى وسط النار ميتة فذهلوا ولم يستجروا بخروا الملك فوقفوا ونكوا لشباها ولغرتها حتى سمع الملك قنبض بقلتق عظيم ودخل ولما رأى جسدها فى وسط النار ميتا ذهل وضرب بيديه
a magnificent feast to his great men, his stewards, and all his troops. Then he thought of the water, the pastes, the perfumes, the good scents and had them brought to her. The wood was placed in an isolated place, where there was no one, and he ordered to set fire to it. She rose, washed her face, her hands, and her feet; she left on her body her inner veils, put on a white, foreign costume, rose, prayed, offered the perfumes, and went to the place of the fire. She closed the door until it was lit; she humbled herself, implored Our Lord Jesus and said: "Lord, you died for me and I am also going to die for you on this day: your mark is imprinted on my face by my beauty; I have become for you a bride and a fiancée; I conjure you, Lord, receive promptly my sacrifice from my hand, receive my soul so that I may prostrate myself before the Virgin, your mother; I come with the seal of my virginity." Then she threw herself into the fire and gave up her soul. Her clothes attached themselves to her body and were not consumed; the fire did not approach her: no hair of her head was burned. After a fairly long time, as she did not come out, the eunuchs opened the door and saw her lying dead in the middle of the fire: they lost their minds and did not dare to inform the king. They remained weeping because of her youth and her exile, so much so that the king heard them. He rose with great trouble, entered, and when he saw her body lying dead in the middle of the pyre, he was distraught, struck his hands one against the other, he said: "In truth, she has troubled my spirit; I have been


[p. 661] {#po.11.p661}
على بعضيم وقال بالحقيقة هذ اقلقت روحى وسخرت بى واحد انها اوقرتنى حملا ثقيلا بايمان واقسام لكى احمل جددها الى كورتها امن للوقت ان يجيل جندها وحتفظوها ويكثنوها بملابس ملوكية واطياب وحملوها كانائمة وذهبوا يا الى ديرها ولما خروا الام1 والخوات خرجوا للفرس الذين احضروها فحدثوهم بكلما جرى ليا ففرحوا بتوليتها وحكمتها وشهادتها ومجدوا الله الذى له المجد الى الابد الرب يرحمنا بصلاتها امين
bewitched by her, and the hardest thing is that she charged me by oath with an arduous task, by making me swear to transport her body to her country." Then he ordered immediately to take away her corpse, to preserve it, to wrap it in royal garments and perfumes. They transported her as a dead person and carried her to her convent. When the Superior and the sisters were informed of it, they went out to meet the procession that was bringing her. They recounted everything that had happened and they rejoiced at the preservation of her virginity, her wisdom, and her martyrdom: they praised God to whom belongs the eternal praise. May the Lord have mercy on us through
اليوم الثانى والعشرون من شهر طونة
her prayers! TWENTY-SECOND OF THE MONTH OF TOUBEH (January 17).
فى هذا اليوم3 تنيح4 القديس التظيمة كوكب البرية؟ واب7 جميع الرهبان؟ انطويوس؟ وكان10 هذا القديس من اهل قمن م1 قبل مص وكانوا؟1 والديه13 مسيحين لما كمل؟! عشرين سنة اعتمد بالمعمودية المقدسة ومن بعد موت والديه؟! فرق جميع مله7!
On this day died the great saint, the star of the desert, the father of all monks, Anthony (Antounyous). This saint was from Qiman, in the south of Egypt (Misr), and his parents were Christians. When he had reached his twentieth year, he received holy baptism. After the death of his parents, he shared


[p. 662] {#po.11.p662}
على الضعفاء والمساكين! وكان له اخت واحدة سلما للعذارى كان محا لنسك4 والانقراد ولم يكن اسم الرهبنة ظهر بل5 كان من اراد ان3 ينفرد يخرج خارج بلده بقليل9 وتفرد"! للنسك وكذلك فعل العظيم انطونيوس وكان الشيطان بحارية!! بالملل والكسل وفضل الخطية وكان يجيب! له شخص امرأة كانا نائمة13 معه وكان يقوى! على هذا جميعه بمعونة السيد المسيح الكائنة معه15 وعد هذا مضى الى قر وسكن فيه وصار يصنع عبادات عظيمة هناك وببد19 باب المكان عليه وكانوا معارفه يفتقدلا وباتو”! بالطعام فلما رأوا الشياطين9! ذلك منه" حسدود واتوا اليه وضريوة!" ضريا كثيرا وجيعا وتركوه مطروحاة” فلما اتوا معارفه يفتقدودة وجدودة على هذا" الحال فحملوا الى الكنيسة” وان الرب38 أعطاء" الشفاء فلما استيقظ لنفسه امرهم ان يحبلوا وسيدوا الى مكانه فلما غلبت الشياطين30 فى هذا اتواة اليه بخالات كثيرة فى زى وحوش وذياب وساع اين
all his goods among the poor and the unfortunate. He had an only sister whom he entrusted to the virgins. He loved asceticism and solitude, but the name of monk had not yet appeared; he who wanted to live as a solitary went out to some distance from his city and isolated himself to devote himself to asceticism. Thus did the great Anthony. Satan attacked him with boredom, laziness, and evil actions: he made him see the image of a woman sleeping with him. But he resisted all that with the help of Our Lord the Messiah who was with him. Then he went to a tomb and lived there: he began to perform great devotions there and closed the door of this place upon himself: his acquaintances went to visit him and brought him food. At this sight the demons hated him, struck him with many painful blows, and left him lying there. When his acquaintances came to visit him, they found him in this state; they transported him to the church and the Lord gave him healing. When he came to himself, he ordered them to take him up and bring him back to his dwelling. The demons, having been thus defeated, came under many


[p. 663] {#po.11.p663}
وعقادب وكل منهم يهم عليه ان ييلكه! وكان يستهزى بهم وقول ليم ان3 كان لكم علق سلطان فواحد منكم يكفينى وعند ذلك3 كانوا يضحكون من قدامه كالدخان واستراح من الالم والتجادب واعطادة الرب7 غلبة على" الشياطين وكان يخبز خزة فى السنة دفعتين وسففه ولم يكن يدع احدا؟ يدخل اليه بل يقفون خارجا وسمعون كلامه واقام عشرين سنة يتعبد بنسك9! عظيم ثم امره!! الرب! ان ينفع13 خنس البشر وتعلميهم4! خوف الله وعبادته فمضى الى الفيوم وثيت اخوة كانواة! هناك وعاد الى دير وفى زمان الشهادة اشتاق ان يصير شهيد وترك16 ديره”! ومضى الى الاسكندرية8! واعترف بالسيد19 السيح فلم يمسكه احدا وكان يفتقد المومنين99 المسجونين! على اسم السيد السيع وحزيهم فلما رأى الحاكم؟ قلة مخافته امر ان لا يظبر راهب بالجملة وهو كان يظبر له مجاهرة ويخاطبه لعله يفتاظ عليه فيعذبه وبضريهة فيصير؟ شهيدا فلم ”7 يكلمه وذلك
forms, with the appearance of wild beasts, wolves, lions, dragons, and scorpions; each of them strove to make him perish, but he mocked them and said to them: "If you had power over me, a single one of you would suffice." Then they vanished before him like smoke, and he was delivered from pain and temptations: the Lord gave him victory over the demons. He baked bread twice a year and dried it; he let no one enter his place, but people stood outside and listened to his words. He remained twenty years adoring God with great fervor. Then the Lord ordered him to be useful to men and to teach them to fear God and to serve him. He went into the Fayoum and strengthened brothers who were there, then he returned to his convent. During the persecution, he desired to be a martyr; he left his convent and went to Alexandria (El-Iskandaryah): he confessed his faith in Our Lord the Messiah, but no one arrested him. He visited the faithful imprisoned for the name of Our Lord the Messiah and consoled them. When the governor saw his lack of fear, he forbade any monk to show himself; but Anthony presented himself to him in public and challenged him in the hope that he would get angry


[p. 664] {#po.11.p664}
ان1 الرب حفظه لمنفعة كثيرين وتديرة من الله عاد الى ديلا كان لاس ثوب شعر ولا يستحم بماء طول. ايام حياته فكثروا الذين باتون اليه وسمعون؟ تعليمه؟ فيسجس من ذلك فامرا الرب بالدخول الى البرية الجوانية فمضى مع قوم عرب الى داخل البرية مسيرة ثلثة ايام حتى وجد8 ماء وقليل؟ قصب وقليل9! نخل فاحب ذلك الموضع ومكن فيه وكانوا العرب يجيبوا له الخز!! وكان فى البرية وحوش كثيرة! مودية وصلاتة طردهم الرب من هناك ولم يعودوا الى البريةة! وكان فى بض الاوقات! يخرج الى ديرا البرانى ويفتقد الاخوة الذين! هناك وبعزييم وبعود الى ديرلا الجوانى وللغ صيته الى19 قسطنطين العظيم7! فكتب8! له «سالة يمدحه وبطلب9! صلواته0ة ففرجحوا الاخوة بكتاب !2 الملك واما هو فلم يليفت الى الكتب بل قال لبم4 هوذا لناآ كتب ملك
against him and would torture him, strike him, and that thus he would be a martyr, but the governor did not speak to him, and this because God was preserving the saint for the good of many men. - By a design of God, he returned to his convent. He was dressed in a hair shirt and during his life never bathed. Those who came to him and listened to his teaching multiplied and he was troubled by it: God ordered him to go into the inner desert. He left with a troop of Arabs ('Arab) until the middle of these solitudes at a distance of three days: he found a spring, some reeds, and a small number of palm trees. This place pleased him and he settled there. The Arabs brought him bread: there were many dangerous wild animals in the desert: at his prayer, the Lord drove them from there and they did not return. At various intervals, he returned to his outer convent, visited the brothers who were there and consoled them, then he returned to his inner convent. His fame reached Constantine (Qostantin) the Great and he wrote him a letter where he praised him and where he asked for his prayers. The brothers rejoiced at the imperial epistle; as for Anthony, he paid no attention to it, but he said: "The king of kings writes to us every day to


[p. 665] {#po.11.p665}
الملوك يوصينا كل يوم ونحن نردها! ولا تلتفت؟ الها والجهد رضى ان يكتب الجواب لما مقلوا عليه الاخوة وقالوا هذا ملك4 بار ومحب؟ للكنيسة6 فكتب7 اليه وعزاه وبارك عليه وكان قد جاء عليه8 الملل؟ سمع صوت يقول له اخرج برأ لترى فخرج ووجد0! ملاكا عليه!! اصطواة! ومتوشح بززار صليب مثل3! الاسكيم وعلى رأسه كوكاس4! كشبه الخودةة! وهو جالس يظفر؟! ثم يقوم”! يصلى ثم يجلس يظفر؟! واتلا صوت9! يقول يا انطونيوس افعل هكذا وانت تستريح فاتخذا! زى النى هو زى؟ الرهبثة من ذلك اليوم وصار يعمل الظفيرة ولم يعود الملاكة ياتى اليه وتنا على خراب البيعة وتسلط البراطقة عليها واعادتها الى طقسها وتنبا على الرهبان وانهم يكثروا ويتزايدواة ثم يسكنوا القرى ويتركوا البرية ونبا على انقضاء الزمان وهو الذى البس مقاريوس زى الرهبنة وعزاا وعرفه بما9 يكون منه ومضى الى عند انا بولا الكبير وهو الذى أهتم بجسده وكفنه
give us recommendations, we push them away, we pay no attention to them." He consented with difficulty to write a reply, for the brothers pressed him with these words: "It is a virtuous emperor who loves the Church." He wrote to him to console and bless him. He was a prey to boredom and he heard a voice that said to him: "Go out and look." He went out and found an angel with a long mantle, girded with a Christian belt, like the monastic garment, and having on his head a cap like a helmet. He was sitting, eyes lowered, then rose to pray and sat down lowering his eyes. A voice reached him saying these words: "Anthony, act thus and you will be at rest." From that day, he took the garment which is that of the monks, and lowered his eyes: the angel did not return. He prophesied the ruin of the Church, the triumph of the heretics over it, its return to its organization. He announced that the monks would multiply, that they would inhabit the cities and would leave the desert. He prophesied about the end of the world. It is he who clothed Macarius (Magäryous) in the religious habit; he consoled him and taught him what would happen to him. He went to find Anbâ Paul (Boulâ) the great;


[p. 666] {#po.11.p666}
بحلة اتناسيوس الرسولى1 ولما حس «القديس3 انطونيون بقرب4 نباحته امن أولاده ان يحقوا جندة واوصاهم ان يعطوا العكاز الذى له8 لمقاريوس والفروة لاتناسيوس والملوطة” الجلد لسرابيون وامتد على الارض واسلم الزوح فخرج فى لقا صفوف الملائكة والقديسين واخذوه9 فى10 موضع النياح اما!! جسدا فاخفوة اولاده كما أوضاهم لانه كان .يعتب على الذين يظهرون. اجساد الشهداء والقديسين حتى ياخذوا عليهم الفضة ويصير ليم بذلك مكسب عاش الى حد"! المبخوخة النظيمةة! ولم يتغير جسده ولا قوته! ولاتنائر شى5! من اسنانه ومضى الى المسيح9! الذى احبه صلاته وشفاعته تكون مع جميعنا”! امين اليوم الثالث18 والعشرون9!
he took care of his body and buried him in the mantle of Athanasius (Atanäsyous) the apostolic. When saint Anthony felt his death approaching, he ordered his sons to hide his body and recommended them to give to Macarius the staff he had, his camel-hair garment to Athanasius, his mantle to Serapion (Saräbyoun); he stretched himself on the earth and gave up his soul. Troops of angels and saints went out to meet him and led him to the place of rest. His sons hid his body as he had recommended to them, for he blamed those who exhibit the bodies of martyrs and saints to withdraw money from them and make gain for themselves. He lived until the limit of extreme old age, without his body or his strength changing, without losing a tooth: he went to find the Messiah who loved him. May his prayer and his intercession be with us all! Amen. TWENTY-THIRD OF THE MONTH OF TOUBEH (January 18).
من شير طوية تيح !* القديس انا بداسيوس وهذا كان من اهل فاو ساكن هو واباته عند رجل صالح
On this day died Anbâ Badâsyous: he was from Phebôou (Fâou); he


[p. 667] {#po.11.p667}
مسبحى حسن السيرة جدا يدعى اسمه بجوش وهذا الرجل لم يرزق ولدا قط سوى ولد واحد فدعا. اسمه يوساب فنشا احدهما وتربى مع الاخر فى منزل واحد ولما تريوا وكبروا قليل من الطفولية كانوا يترددوا الى مجمع دير ابينا القديس ابو بخوم وينظروا حسن عادة الرجبان النساك المجاهدين المقيمين هناك ومعلوا يطلبوا منهم ويرغبوا اليهم قائلين اقبلونا عندكم من احجل السيد المسيح لنتعبد نحن ايضا كعاداتكم فاجابوا تلك الاباء القديسين قائلين يكون كذلك كحسب ما سأتم وهكذا كان قبول القديسين عند اولائك الابرار وكان بالدير مقيم فى تلك الايام رجل موط يدعى اسمه بولس شبه بالرسل وان هذا القديس انا بداسيوس تردد اليه ذات يوم وهو فى قلايته واختلايه وقال له يا ابى انا بولس عرقى وقول لى كيف يكون خلاصى وبا اصنع لكى يرحمنى الرب اما الاب انا بولس فانه احابه قائلا له يكون هذا معروفا عندك واعلم ان وصيتين قالبما مخلصنا الرحيم فى انجيله الطاهر وهما يكفيانا نحن وجميع المسيحيين اذا ما.هما حفظوهما واقتصروا على العمل بهما قال له القديس انا بداسيوس وما هما ايها الاب المشفق فاحابه القديس انا بولى
lived with his parents near a virtuous Christian, of good life, who was named Bâdjouch. This man had no other children than a son whom he had called Yousâb: they grew up and were raised together in the same dwelling. When they were raised and had come out a little from childhood, they often went to the convent of our father saint Pachomius (Bakhoum) and saw the beauty of the adoration of the devout and zealous monks who lived there. They expressed their envy and their desire to them in these terms: "Receive us among you, because of Our Lord the Messiah, so that we may adore him also as you do." The holy fathers replied to them: "It will be as you ask", and thus the two saints were accepted among these virtuous people. At that time, lived in the convent a blessed man named Paul (Boulos), like the apostles. This saint Anbâ Badâsyous went to find him one day when he was in his cell and his retreat and said to him: "My father Anbâ Paul, instruct me and tell me how I can be saved and what I must do so that the Lord may have pity on me." - The father Anbâ Paul replied to him: "That will be known to you: know that there are two recommendations that our merciful Savior has made in his pure Gospel: they suffice for us and for all Christians if one observes them and if one limits oneself to acting according to them." - The saint Anbâ Badâsyous asked him: "And what are they, O compassionate father?" - The saint Anbâ Paul said to him: "The Lord your God is unique; you


[p. 668] {#po.11.p668}
الرب الاهك واحدا هو وتحب الرب الاهك من كل قلبك ومن كل تفسك ومن كل قوتك ومن كل افكارك والوصية الثانية التى تشبها تحب قريبك كحبك نفك فبذه هى خير وافضل من جميع الذبايح المحرقات! حسب ما قال الرب سبحانه لكاتب وانا اعترنى لك انك اذا ما حفظت وتمسكت ياتين الوصيتين قد تتكى وتاكل مع الرسل الاطبار فى يوم الحكم الاعظم ولا يكون قلبك يق بالاسم والعكل لكن تامل ما قاله يعقوب اخو الرب اذ يقول فى رسالته الجامعة كل افتخار مثل هذا خيث لان منا اليوم فى هولاء الرهبان الذين تشاهدهم لابين هذا الاسكيم المقدس من قد شهد ليم السمايين انهم قد بلغوا الى حد غطة الاسكيم الذى لبسوا وطوى أيضا للذين كملوا سيرتهم وهم علمانين اخير وافضل من الذين ارتبوا باسكيم الرهبنة ولم يكملوا فرائضها وحقوتها الويل لاوليك الذين هم على مثل هذا الحال لقد كان اخير ليم لو لم يولدوا فى هذا العالم البئة ليتهم يظفرون برحمة فى يوم حكم العدل حيث9 يعرضونم امام ذلك المنبن العظيم المرهوب وهم قياما
will love the Lord your God with all your heart, with all your soul, with all your strength and with all your thoughts; the second recommendation which resembles the first is: You will love your neighbor as yourself. That is better and is worth more than all sacrifices, all burnt offerings according to what the Lord - may he be praised! - said to the scribe. I declare to you that if you observe and if you keep these prescriptions, you will sit and you will eat with the virtuous apostles on the day of the supreme judgment. Let not your heart trust in the name and the form; but reflect on the words of James (Ya'qoub), brother of the Lord, when he says in his catholic epistle: All vanity like this is detestable for today, among us, there are monks whom you see clothed in this sacred habit, for whom the celestial spirits testify that they have reached the limit of happiness attached to the wearing of the habit that they have put on. Happy also those who have led a perfect life, even though they were of the world; they are better and more deserving than those who are bound by the monastic habit and who do not fulfill its duties and its obligations. Woe to those who are thus: it would be better for them that they had not been born into this world. May they obtain mercy on the day of the judgment of


[p. 669] {#po.11.p669}
عراة ونال كل واحد منهم القصاصس عن جميع ما صنع خيرا كان ام شرا والان ياانى فاحتبس فى موضعك منفردا مع نفسك وكون ملازما للوحدة جميع ايام حياتك من غير ان تدع احدا من الناس يعلم كيف يكون حهادك وتعبدك لله ولا تظهر للناس شيا من عملك البتة في صومك وصلاتك واذا ما أنت صنعت رحمة مع سكين فلا تدع احدا يعلم بك ليلا يشيع اجرك وبخيب تعبك ولا تظن يااخى المحبوب ان هذه الوصايا التى اعلمك يا هى لى ولا من عندى معاذ الله من ذلك لكنها وصايا رنا يسوع المسيح على الحقيقة التى علمها لرسله الاطبار فى انجله المقدس قائلا اذا صمتم فلا تكونوا كالمرايين لانهم يبسون وحوهيم ليظهروا للناس صيامهم الحق اقول لكم انهم قد اخذوا اجرهم وانت فاذا صنت اغل وحبك وادهن رأسك ليلا يظهر للناس صيامك لكن لابيك عالم السر وابوك الذى .يرى السر يجازيك علانية! وانت اذا ما صليت فادخل الى مخدعك واغلق بابك وصلى لابيك فى السر وابوك الذى يرى السر بجازيك علانية وقال ايضا اذا صليتم
justice when they will appear before this august and formidable tribunal; they will be standing and naked; each of them will receive a remuneration for all his actions, good or bad. My son, keep yourself isolated with your soul in your place; attach yourself to solitude all the days of your life, without letting anyone know your efforts and your adoration of God; do not show anything to people of your actions when you fast or you pray; what you do by compassion for a poor person, do not let anyone know it, for fear of diminishing your reward and rendering your efforts vain; do not believe, my dear son, that these precepts that I teach you are mine or come from me; God forbid! but they are the precepts of Our Lord Jesus the Messiah; it is the truth that he taught to his pure apostles in his holy Gospel: When you fast, he says, do not be like the hypocrites, for they have an austere face to show people that they are fasting. In truth I tell you: they have received their reward; but you, when you fast, wash your face and anoint your head to make it seen that you are fasting not to people, but to your Father who knows the secrets: and your Father who knows the hidden things will reward you publicly. When you pray, enter into your room, close your door, and address in secret your prayer to your Father: he who knows the secrets will reward you publicly. He also said: When you


[p. 670] {#po.11.p670}
فلا تكونوا كمثل المرايين لانهم يظنون انه يسمع ليم بكثرة كلامم فلا تتشبهوا بهم لان اباكم السمابى عالم بما تحتاجون اليه قبل ان تسألوه وهكذا تكون صلاتكم ابانا الذى فى السماوات فليتقدس اسمك تاتنى ملكوتك تكون مسرتك كما فى السماء كذلك على الارض خبزنا اعطينا اليوم واغفى لنا ما علينا كما انا ايضا نغفر لمن لنا عليه ولا تدخلنا التجارب بل نجينا من الشرير فان لك القوة والمجد الى ابد الابدين امين! وقال ايضا اذا ما صنعت رحمة فلا تدع شمالك تعلم ما صنعت يمينك لكيما تكون رحمتك فى خفية وابوك الذى ينظر السر فيجازيك علانية والان يااخى فاتفرد فى قلايتك مع نفسك ولازم الصوم والصلاة والوحدة ولا تدع احدا من الناس يتطلع على عادتك ولا يعلم كيف عملك لا من العلمانيين ولا من الرهبان وسوف تعاين مجد الله فلما قال هذا انا بولس خرج من عندة وهو يمجد الله على كلام التعاليم الرسولية والوصايا الانجيلية التى اوعزها اليه ومن ذلك اليوم التزم القديس انا بداسيوس قلايته وانفرد فيها وحده واخذ ان يقمع جنده بالصوم والصلاة مستمرا على الدوام ولم يأكل شى يخرج منه دم وكان اذا ما بلغ الى اوان
pray, do not be like the babblers, for they believe that he only listens to them because of the abundance of their words. Do not resemble them, for your heavenly Father knows what you need before you ask him. Let your prayer be this: Our Father who art in heaven, hallowed be thy name, thy kingdom come; thy will be done on earth as it is in heaven; our bread, give it to us today; forgive us our offenses as we forgive those who have offended us; lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil, for thou possessest the power and the glory in the centuries of centuries. Amen - He also said: When you do a charity, let not your left hand know what your right hand has done, so that your generosity remains hidden, and your Father who sees the secrets will reward you publicly. Now, my brother, isolate yourself in your cell with yourself: attach yourself to fasting, to prayer, to solitude; let no one know your adoration; let no one, neither people of the world, nor religious, know your actions, and you will contemplate the glory of God." When Anbâ Paul had pronounced these words, Anbâ Badâsyous left him praising God for the apostolic teachings and the evangelical precepts that Anbâ Paul had preached to him. From that day, the saint did not leave his hermitage again; he isolated himself there and applied himself to taming his body by fasting and


[p. 671] {#po.11.p671}
الصيف ينضجع فى الح وإذا ما انتهى الى فصل الشتاء يجمل رقاده فى الظل والندى وكل ما يكتسبه من شغل يديه بتعه الا يبقى منه شى النفسه اسوى قدر ما يكتفى به من القوت فقط انوا الاخوة يطلبون اليه مرار كثيرة قائلين تحضر عندنا من اجل الله وبارك فى مايدتنا وتاكل معنا اما هو فكان يقول ليم بعظم تواضعه ما يكفانى منكم انكم احنتم التي وقلتمونى عندكم انا الباشى الشقي حتى التمس ما يفوق مقدارى ثم انه مكث يقمع جسده بالنسك والكد والتعب ولما انتهى الى الصوم المقدس قال فى قله هذا الاربمين المقدسة فينبغى لى ازيد فها على نسكى وعادتى بالاكثر وانظن هل قبانى الله ورضى علي وتعهدنى بكثرة رحمته ومرار كثيرة كان يصنع ارعمأية صلاة فى النهار وثلث مآية فى الليل وكان يقول ياسيدى يسوع المسيح ان انت رضيت علتي وتباتنى لا افتر ولا اتخلى مما انا الان مقيما عليه الى النفس الاخير وكان رجلا اعرج مخلع اليدين والرحجلين مقعد يمن من بطن امه يتردد الى الدير فى اكثر اوقاته بامانة مضمر فى نفسه قائلا ان الرب قريب من جميع الذين يدعو وكل الذين يبتهلون اليه تملوب عادقة ويصنع مسرة اتقيلا ويستجيب طلبات الذين يرغبون اليه ويخلصهم من جميع احزام
prayer, constantly firm: he ate nothing from which blood came out. When he arrived at the summer season, he lay down in the heat; when winter came, he placed his bed in the shade and the dew. All that he earned with difficulty from the work of his hands, he kept nothing of it except the price of what he needed for his food. The brothers asked him repeatedly: "For God's sake, come among us; bless our table by your presence; eat with us." As for him, he replied to them in his excessive humility: "It is not enough to have showered me with benefits by receiving me among you, me unhappy and miserable, for me to obtain what is above my worth!" - He continued to tame his body by asceticism, fatigue, work. When they arrived at the holy fast, he said to himself in his heart: "It is fitting that during these forty holy days, I redouble my devotion and adoration. I will see if God welcomes me, if he is satisfied with me and if I can count on the greatness of his mercy." Often he performed four hundred prayers by day and three hundred by night, and he said: "Lord Jesus the Messiah, if you are satisfied with me and if you welcome me, I will not abandon and I will not cease my way of life until my last breath." There was a lame man, weak in the hands and feet, infirm since his mother's womb, who frequented the convent very often, thinking in himself: "The Lord is near to all those who call upon him: when one supplicates him with a pure heart and when one executes his will, he accomplishes the desire of his devotees,


[p. 672] {#po.11.p672}
وشدائدهم والرب يحفظ جميع المحبين لاسمه كقول داوود الحسن فى المرتلين اذ يقول الرب يتم جميع طلبتك ويعطيك كمثل قلبك ويكمل جميع مصرتك لما كان فى وقت الساعة التاسعة من انبار حضر ذلك الاعرج الممخلع الى باب الدير كجارى عادته ليسئل الصدقة من الرهان القديسين الذين هناك اما القديس فخرج للقلا بما اتيس عند من البركة وذلك المقعد راكب دابته اذ كان لا يستطيع يمشى على رحليه منذ قط قال له القديس ايها الرجل هل, ولدوك هكذا احاب قائلا له ها قد ترانى ياسيدى الاب وتشاهد ضعفى ويسكنتى انا اقسم عليك لله الذى اياه تيد كى ترشم بالصليب المقدس يدى ورجلى بيدك الزكية فاما القديس انا بداسيوس فانه قال لذلك الاعرج الرب الاله القوى سبحانه القادر .على كل شى الذى لا يعر عليه شى كما وهب الشفاء لذلك الاعرج المقعد المخلع برسوليه العظيمين بطرس ويوحنا يهب لك الشفاء ياابنى ويتعطف عليك برأفته ورحمته وللوقت امتدت بدالا ورحلاه واستقامت اعضلا كانه لم يناله ضر البته ولم الم منذ قط ونبض صحيحا مستويا يصلى ويسبح الله ويعطى المجد لقدرته واذا رجل اعمى مولود بعينيه
grants the requests of those who desire something from him, he saves them from all their sorrows and all their miseries; the Lord guards all those who love his name, following the beautiful word of David (Dâoud) in the Psalms, when he says: The Lord will grant all your requests and will give you according to your heart; he will accomplish all your desires." When the ninth hour of the day arrived, this infirm lame man stood at the door of the convent following his habit to ask for alms from the holy monks who were there. As for the saint, he went out to make him experience the blessing that was in him: this paralytic had mounted his beast of burden, for he could never walk on his feet. The saint said to him: "Man, were you born thus?" He replied to him: "Thus you see me, O holy father, and you contemplate my weakness and my misery; I conjure you, by the God whom you serve, to make the sign of the holy cross on my hands and my feet with your pure hand." The saint Anbâ Badâsyous replied to this lame man: "Just as the Lord, the strong God - praise to him! - who can do everything, to whom nothing is difficult, gave healing to this lame, weak and infirm man, by the means of his two great apostles Peter (Botros) and John (Youhannä), in the same way he will give you healing, my son, and will be benevolent to you by his kindness and his mercy." Immediately, his hands and his feet lengthened; his limbs straightened as if he had never experienced either evil or pain. He stood up healthy and straight, prayed, gave thanks to God and celebrated his power. There was a blind man from birth who also presented himself to ask


[p. 673] {#po.11.p673}
الثنتين قد حضر هو ايضا ليسئل الصدقة فلما سمع بضجيج ذلك الاعرج اذ هو يجرى ويتقفز الى كل الجبات بغاية الفرح طفق هو ايضا يتضرع الى الله وسئل قائلا انا اقسم عليك بلله الذى انت له عبد كى يتعطف علتي انا ايضا وينظر الي بعين الرأفة والرحمة وتطاب الى الله من اجلى لهب لى ضو بصرى ويتحنن على مسكنتى وشقوتى بصلواتك المقبولة امامه وكرامة منزلتك عند وانا ارجو ان الله ما يرد سوألك فى ولا خب طلبتك لاجلى بل يترااف عاتي ويرحمنى قال له القديس عند ذلك انا اسئل الاله الكلمة النى تجسد من مرتمريم العذراء البتول الزكية ذلك الذى تن على « الاعمى المولود وفتح عينيه واضاء بصيرته بيده العزيزة الالبية حين تفل على الارض وصنع من تفلته طينا وطلى به عينيه ان يترااف عليك انت ايضا ويفتح عينيك وبضى بصيرتك كمثل ذلك وللوقت انفتحت عينيه واضت بصيرته وامتلاه من كل الافراح والببجة الروحانية ثم دخلا كلاهما الى الدير الاعرج والاعمى وارهجا ذلك المكان كله بالفرح قائلين تعالوا وانظروا رجل الله كيف شفانا واوهب لنا الصحة باسم الرب بالبذه الاية الباهرة السمابية والاعجوبة الطاهرة الالبية حضرئا الى هاهنا فى طلب قوت" جسدانى فتلنا نعمة الصحة وموهة الشفاء
for alms. When he heard the cries of this paralytic who was running and leaping on all sides in the excess of his joy, he also began to humble himself before God and to implore him saying: "I conjure you by the God whom you serve, to ask him to have compassion on me and to cast upon me a look of kindness and mercy. Ask God for me to restore my light and to have pity on my distress and my misery, by your answered prayer and the nobility of your rank near him. I hope that God will not reject your request in my favor and will not render vain your supplication for me, but that he will show himself compassionate and merciful toward me." - The saint replied to him: "I ask God the Word who became incarnate in Mary the immaculate Virgin, who had pity on the blind man from birth, opened his eyes and made his gaze shine by his powerful and divine hand, when he spat on the ground and made with his saliva a mud with which he anointed his eyes; I ask him to show himself benevolent also toward you, to open your eyes, to make your gaze shine like that one." Immediately his eyes opened, his gaze shone: he was filled with all sorts of joy and a spiritual beauty. Then the blind man and the lame man both entered the convent and filled this place with joy saying: "Come and see the man of God! how he has healed us and restored our health in the name of the Lord! O brilliant and celestial miracle! O pure and divine prodigy! We were here in search of bodily food and we have


[p. 674] {#po.11.p674}
الروحانى عجيبا هو الله فى قديسيه وممجدا فى راى اصفيلد يالبذا القوات العظيمة والمواهب الروحانية الجسمية التى استها الله سحانه وافاضها على هذا القديس الار المصطفى فطوبى للبطن الذى حمله والثديين الذين ارشعاد ليا الانسان المخلص الذى وجدت امه السبيل لتلده حيدا يامن قد صعدت صلواته ومراحمه تذكارا له قدام الرب الاله ولما ذاع خير هذا القديى فى الدير من اجل مواهب الغفاء التى كان الله يجريه على يديه سألوا الاخوة باجتهاد ورغبوا الي الله كى يلبسوه اسكيم الرينة وانه خرج من الدير سرا فى الليل ومضى واختفى فى بيت امرأة ارملة من اهل فاو اقام هناك اسبوعين وهو مختفى فى بيت تلك الامرأة القديسة الصالحة بسيدييا ولما كان ثالث عيد الفصح المجيد خرج من بيت تلك الامرأة القديسة وعاد راجعا الى قلايته ومكثوا الاخوة يرغبون اليه ويلحخون لاجل هذا الامر اما هو فقال ليم اغفروا لى ابا الاخوة الاحياء فاتنى بعد لم ابلغ الى هذا المقدار الرفيع الذى هو اسكيم الرهبنة الملايكى لانى انا انسان كثير المناقص جدا وهذا الامر الذى تدعونى اليه ليس هو امر هين هكذا لكنه يريد اناس ابرار صالحين أخبار وقد نشوا فى اعمال مرضية وعبادة ملايكية وسيرة سكية زكية ليس فيا شى من
received the benefit of health and the gift of mystical healing. God is wonderful in his saints and praiseworthy in the spirit of his stainless servants. What immense forces! What spiritual and bodily gifts with which God - may he be praised! - has gratified in advance and showered this virtuous and chosen saint! Happy the womb that carried him! (Happy) the breasts that nursed him! O you our Savior whose mother found the way to bear you gloriously! O you whose prayers and acts of mercy rise in commemoration to the Lord God!" When the story of this saint relative to the gifts of healing that God had accomplished through his hands spread in the convent, the brothers asked him with ardor while supplicating God to put on the monastic habit. At night, he went out in secret from the convent and went to hide in the house of a widow of Phebôou (Fâou). He remained there two weeks, hidden in the house of this saint and virtuous woman Basidyyâ. When the third day of the glorious feast of Easter arrived, he went out of the house of this saintly woman and returned to his cell. The brothers begged him with insistence for this matter, but he replied to them: "Forgive me, my dear brothers, but I have not reached this high degree which is the celestial monastic habit, for I am a man burdened with many faults and that to which you call me is not an easy thing, but it requires stainless, virtuous, excellent people, who


[p. 675] {#po.11.p675}
العيب ولا دنس ولا تكون قلوبهم ماثلة الى شى من امور هذا العالم الزائل: ولا تظنوا بااخوة ان موهية الشفاء الذى ادركت الاعرج حين مشى والاعمى حيث نظر الى كلا معاذ الله لكن امانتهما التي خلصتهما ولما قال تركو ولم يعودوا يلوا عليه بل مكث مقيم على حاله قائلين كما تريد اصنع مسري الله وتحرز لنفسك جدا من هذا العالم ليلا بغرك لانه قد اغر كثيرا اما هو فقال لهم يكون لى ذلك ان اخلص بمعونة صلواتكم وسكث هذا القديس انا بداسيوس يزيد على عباداته ويضاعف صومه وصلاواته وسهره ولم يكن له تلاوة سوى هذا الكلام الواحد يقوله ليلا ونبارا اللبم اصنع رحمة مع نفسى الائسة الثقية عند وقوفى ين يديك من بعد هذا الخطوب توحعت ارسانوى زوحة ارخن كبير من اهل فاو فى جانبى وجها بمرض الشقيقة حتى كادت حدقتا اليمين تنقلع وتخرج لو لم يشدوها وبضطوها بالنقاب والعصائب والرفائد واقامت فى هذ العقوبة اسبوعين حتى صارت كالذين هم فى سكرات الموت واحضر اليا زوحا كثيرا من الاطباء ولم يالبا راحة ولا عافية فاعلموها اناس قائلين هوذا انسان يدير القديس ابو بخوم يدعا اسمه بداسيوس قد شفا
have grown up in works pleasing to God, in angelic adoration, in a chaste and pure life, without any sin, without any stain; whose hearts do not lean toward the interests of this perishable world. Do not believe, my brothers, that it is necessary to attribute to me the gift of the healing of this lame man when he walked, of this blind man when he saw; no, God forbid; but it is their faith that saved them." When he had spoken thus, they left him and did not insist anymore with him. He remained as he was, while they said to him: "Accomplish the will of God as you will and protect well your soul against this world, for fear that it may deceive you, for it is extremely deceitful." - As for him, he said to them: "It will happen that I will save myself through your prayers." This saint Anbâ Badâsyous began to redouble his devotions, to reiterate his fasts, his prayers and his vigils; he recited only this single word that he repeated night and day: "My God, have pity on my guilty and perverse soul when I appear before you." - After these events, Arsinoé (Arsânoui), wife of the first magistrate of Phabôou, suffered from migraine on both sides of her face, so much so that her right pupil had almost been torn out and would have come out if it had not been tied and maintained with a veil, bandages, and pads. She remained two weeks thus tormented, so much so that she resembled those who are a prey to the throes of death. Her husband had many doctors come, but they procured her neither rest nor healing. People gave him this information: "In the convent of saint Anbâ Bakhoum,


[p. 676] {#po.11.p676}
الاعرج واضاء بصيرة الاعمى اولتك الذين كانوا يسألون الصدقة فان ذهب زوك بك اليه ليرشم وحبك بعلامة الصليب فانك عند ذلك تجدى الراحة لوقتك وتثالى الثشقاء بل دعى بعلك ينطلق اليه أولا ويستدعيه الى خارج الدير واذا هو خرج برا ورائتيه انتى يتحدث معه انبضى سرعة وبادرى اليه واسكى يديه وسحيبما على وجهك فيسكن الضريان لوقته وينعم الله عليك بالصحة وتنالى موهبة الشفاء وتتعافى بصلواته المقبولة الزكية فلما سمعت عرفت زوجها الارخن بذلك ونه انطلق الى الدير وابتداً يقرع باب خزانة القديس وللوقت خرج للقلا ولما رأ امسك بيده الطاهرة وقبلبا وكلمه باحتيال روحانى قائلا تفضل ايا الاب القديس واخرج معى الى خارج برا باب الدير حتى اعلمك هذه الحاجة. الضرورية فمشى معه القديس للوقت وهو كالخروف الوديع القلب الذى لا غشا فيه وفيما هو يتحدث معه واذا ارسانوى زوحة الارخن قد حضرت الى الوسط ومسكت يد القديس وقبلتها وسحت با وحها قائلة رحمة صلواتك المقبولة الركية تدركنى انا الائسة الشقية لانى تعبت جدا وللوقت عوفيت واستراحت من جميع تعبا وبريت من شدة وجا
There is a man called Badâsyous who healed a lame man and restored the brightness of sight to a blind man, both of whom had come to beg for alms. If your husband takes you to him so that he may make the sign of the cross upon your face, you will immediately find relief and obtain your healing; but rather, ask your husband to speak to him first and call him out of the convent; when he is outside and you see him conversing with him, go in all haste, present yourself before him, seize his hands, and rub them upon your face; the pain will calm down at once, God will grant you health, and you will obtain the gift of your healing: you will be healed thanks to his answered and pure prayers.” When she had heard these words, she informed her husband the magistrate. He went to the convent and began to knock at the door of the saint's cell. The latter came out at once to meet him. When the magistrate saw him, he seized his pure hand, kissed it, and spoke to him using a spiritual ruse: "My holy father," he said, "do me the honor of coming out with me beyond the gate of the convent so that I may inform you of this urgent matter." Immediately, the saint went with him like a lamb, simple of heart and without guile. While he was conversing with him, Arsinoe, the magistrate's wife, intervened, seized the saint's hand, kissed it, and rubbed her face with it, saying: "May I experience the mercy of your answered and pure prayers, I, a guilty and miserable woman! For I am suffering extremely." Immediately she was healed, relieved of all her pains, and delivered from


[p. 677] {#po.11.p677}
وذهب بها بعلا الى منزله وهو يمجد الله صانع العجائب فى قديسيه وعند ذلك ذاع الخبر فى المدينة لاجله وقدموا اليه جميع الذين بهم اصناف الامراض والاوجاع المختلفة فشفاهم الجميع باسم الرب وعند ذلك قام للوقت وانطلق الى عند انا يساب اخود الذى كان قد حاء صحته الى الدين وقال له قم نا يااخى نمضى الى الصعيد الى بلاد قفط ونقيم بقية ايامنا هناك حيث لا نجد فيه احد يعوقنا عن كمال عبادتنا وكان يذكر كلام الرسول بولس القائل اننى لست التمس مجدا من الناس ثم مكث متفرغا للصوم والصلاة والسهر والنسك والعادة العظيمة وهو يخفى ذلك عن الناس والله بعلنه لكل احد ويرهنه وقيل ايضا عنه ان اناس اقاموا اياما كثيرة هم يحفرون فى بير ولم يظهر لهم فيها اثر ماء البتة ولما اتفق عبور هذا القديس عليهم بتدبير من الله سحانه طلبوا اليه قائلين نحن نقسم عليك بالله الذى انت له متعبد كى ترشم هذا البير بعلامة الصليب المقدس وللوقت بسط يديه ورفع عينيه الى السماء وصلى ورشم البين بمثال الصليب ولم يتباعد منهم سوى مقدار رمية سهم حتى فاض يبنوع عظيم وهوذا هو علامة باقية الى يومنا هذا ولما ذاع
her excessive sufferings. Her husband brought her back to her home, praising God who performs miracles through his saints. The report of this spread throughout the city; they brought to him all those who suffered from all kinds of diseases or various pains, and he healed them all in the name of the Lord. Then he arose in haste and went to Anbâ Yosäb, his brother, in whose company he had come to the convent, and said to him: "My brother, let us rise and leave for Upper Egypt (Es-Sa'id), in the city of Keft (Qift); we will spend the rest of our days there, for we will find no one there to distract us from fully performing our devotions." He mentioned the words of the apostle Paul (Boulos): "I do not seek glory from people." He remained there, giving himself entirely to fasting, prayer, vigils, devotions, and ardent piety. He hid it from people, but God divulged it to all and made him known. It is also told of him that people spent many days digging in a well without a trace of water appearing. When, by the design of God—may He be praised!—the saint passed by them, they addressed this request to him: "We conjure you, by the God whom you worship, to mark this well with the sign of the holy cross." Immediately, he stretched out his hands, lifted his eyes toward heaven, prayed, and traced the image of the cross upon the well. He had not moved away from them the distance of a bowshot when an immense stream overflowed, and this sign has lasted until now. When the reputation of this saint had spread everywhere, they brought to him a young man on whose neck an ulcer had grown;


[p. 678] {#po.11.p678}
خر هذا القديس بكل مكان احضروا اليه صبيا قد طلع فى رقبته خراج وهو ملفوف بعصابة على تلك السلعة التى فى عنقه ليلا يراها احد من الناس لانه كان مرضا رديا بالاكثر يسمى من الاطاء مرض الخازير واتفق ان والدة هذا الطفل صادفت هذا القديس وهو مجتاز فى الطريق وابنها معها فطلبت اليه قائلا من اجل الله ايها القديس ارحم هذا الطفل واطلب من الله ليشفيه من شدة وجعه ثم انها كشفت له حيث وجع الطفل واورته ايند فوضع يده عليه وقال الرب ينوع المسيح طبيب الاتقاس والاجساد القادر سبحائه على كل شى يهب لك الثفاء ياابنى وللوقت حماته والدته وانطلقت الى بيتها واكشفت عنه العصابة فلم تجد للوجع اثر بالجملة بل عوفى كانه لم يناله مكرود فابتهجت عند ذلك بالفرح .ومجدت الله صانع العجائب فى قديسيه واصفيلا اما القديس انا بداسيون فانه مضى الى اننا يوساب وقال له صلى يااخى لانى اظن ان ايامى قد اقتريت لكيما امضى فى سبيل جميع اباى ولبذا انا ارغب اليك ان تذكرنى فى صلواتك بلا فنور حتى اعبر النهر الناري الجارى قدام كرسي ديان العدل والزيانية المختلفين الوجوه الذين فى الطريق فقال له القديى انا يوساب ماذا رايت يااخى حتى قلت هذا فاما هو فقال له كان منى هذ
a bandage wrapped this affliction so that no one would see it; it was the horrible disease that doctors call scrofula. It happened that the mother of this child met this saint while he was passing on the road; she had her son with her. She addressed this request to him: "By God, O saint, have pity on this child and ask God to heal him of this extreme suffering." Then she uncovered the place of the child's disease and showed it to him. He put his hand on him and said: "May the Lord Jesus the Messiah, physician of souls and bodies, all-powerful—may He be praised!—give you healing, O my son." Immediately his mother took him and brought him to the house; she removed the bandage and found no trace of the affliction; he was healed as if no disease had ever touched him. She was joyful and praised God who performs miracles through his saints. As for Saint Anbâ Badäsyous, he went to find Anbâ Yousâb and said to him: "Pray, my brother, for I believe that my days are near the moment when I will follow the path of all my fathers: that is why I pray you to mention me unceasingly in your prayers so that I may cross the river of fire that flows before the throne of the Just Judge and the demons of various faces that are on the road." The saint Anbâ Yousâb asked him: "What have you seen, my brother, to speak thus?" He answered him: "It happened to me this


[p. 679] {#po.11.p679}
الليلة لما صنعت صلواتى المفترضة على العادة وانضجت قليل فدخل الى انسانا مباب عنليم المجد جدا وقال لى_بداسيوسن بداسيوس أهتم بثانك واحسن الغاية بسيرك فقد كمات ايامك لكيما ندعوك الى عندنا وناخذك الينا والان يااخى فلا تتوانى عن المجى لافتقادى لعلمك انا قد تربينا بعضنا مع بعض فى دير واحد ولم يكن يننا افتراق ولليوم الاخر توجع القديس انا بداسيوس ولزم الفراش واشتد عليه القلق جدا وبعد قليل حضر اليه القديس انا يوساب فقال له يااخى الم اقول لك ان ايامى قد اقتريت وقد افتقدنى الرب وحض وقت زوالى فاستودعك لله يااخى الحبيب الى ان يجانى الرب مستحق النظر الى قدسك فى هذا الدهن الاتى فقال له القديس انا يوساب تريد ان تقول لنا كلمة نذكرك با وما سوف يكون بعد وفاتك فقال له القديسن بداسيوس ماذا اقول لك ياابى القديس اذا ما انا مضيت الى الرب ووجدت عند دالة ورحمة انا اسئله ان لا يعطى طريق للبرير ان ياتوا الى هذد الديار زمانا طويلا وهذه علامة تكون لى انى ان وجدت دلة عند الرب سوف ياتى سحا فى جو البوا يوم وفاتى ولما كان ليلة
night, when I had finished my prescribed prayers according to custom and had rested a little, that a formidable and majestic man entered my room and said to me: Badâsyous, Badâsyous! think of your state and make every effort for a good journey, for your days are fulfilled, so that we may call you to us and take you with us. Now, my brother, do not delay in executing my desire, for you know that we were raised together in one convent and that there has never been any separation between us." The next day, the saint Anbâ Badâsyous felt pain and could not leave his bed; his trouble increased greatly; shortly after, the saint Anbâ Yousäb appeared, and he said to him: "My brother, had I not told you that my days were near? The Lord has claimed me; the moment of my end has arrived; I bid you farewell, my dear brother, until the Lord makes me worthy to see your holiness in the future world." The saint Anbâ Yousäb answered him: "We desire that you tell us a word that will remind us of you; what will happen after your death?" - "What shall I tell you, holy father?" said the saint Anbâ Badâsyous. "When I go to find the Lord and when I have found indulgence and mercy near him, I will ask him not to give the Berbers the means to come to this convent for a long time: this will be for me the sign that I have found indulgence with the Lord: a cloud will come into the air on the day of my death." The night of the 21st of the month


[p. 680] {#po.11.p680}
الحادى والعشرين من شبن طوبه عيد سيدتنا مرتمريم العذراء وافى اليه رئيس الملائكة واعطان السلام ومدح سعيه المختار وحسن سيرته الفاضلة واوعده بمواعيد حميلة وبشلا بكرامات جزيلة وقال له اعلم ان الرب قد ارسلنى اليك كيما اعزيك وانجيك واذهب بك الى مساكن الفرحين ولما قال له رئيس الملائكة رافاييل هذا صعد الى السموات للوقت وفى اليوم الثالث والعشرين من شهر طوبه تنيح ابينا القدين. اننا بداسيوس واسام الروح فى يد الله الحى وعند ذلك خرج للقاله جموع كبيرة من طفمات الملائكة الاطيار ويسبحون وبرلون قدام نفسه الى ان اصعدوها بالفرح ولتهليل الى علو السموات ثم اجتمعوا الى جسدد واقبرو ببيعته حيث كان مقامه وكمال صيرلا وحياده وظهر من جد قوانين واشفية حتى ان كلمن يتردد الى بيعته يبرا من جميع اوجاعه الرب الاله بنوأله وصلواته وشفاعته يغفر خطايانا امين
of Toubeh, the day of the feast of Our Lady the Virgin Mary, the chief of the angels arrived, who greeted the saint, praised his virtuous existence and his life full of merits, made him beautiful promises, and announced numerous graces to him. "Know," he said, "that the Lord has sent me to you to console you, to save you, and to bring you into the dwellings of the blessed." When the chief of the angels, Raphael (Râfäyil), had spoken these words, he immediately ascended back toward the heavens. On the 23rd of the month of Toubeh, the saint Anbâ Badäsyous expired and rendered his soul into the hands of the living God. Then a great quantity of choirs of pure angels came out to meet him, praising God and chanting before his soul until they made it ascend with joy and gladness to the highest heavens. Then they gathered around his body and buried it in his church where he used to place himself and where his constancy and his perfect efforts had been manifested. His body produced cures and healings, so much so that whoever frequented his church was healed of all his ailments. May the Lord God forgive us our faults at his request, by his prayers and his intercession! Amen.
فى! هذا اليوم استشهد القديس العظيم طيماتاوين الرسول هذا القديس كان مولده
On this day took place the martyrdom of the great saint * Timothy (Timâtâous)


[p. 681] {#po.11.p681}
وتربيته من مدينة لسطرة وكان ابوه يونانى يعبد الكواكب وامه بهودية تحت ظل الشريعة فلما بشر الرسول فى لسطرة وسمع هذا القديس تعليمه ثم رأى الايات التى يفعلبا الله تعالى على يدا التى تبهر العقلاء وتعجز الفلاسفة عن مثلبا دخل الى مجلسه وامن على يده بالسيد المسيح وتعمد بالثالوث المقدس ورفض البة ابيه وشرائعة وترك العمل بشريعة امه ايضا ثم تتلمذ للرسول وبعه فى اسفار كثيرة وتعب معه ونال شدائد عديدة واحزان كثيرة جدا وانتدبه الرسول اسقفا على مدينة افس فلما دخل فيها بشر باسم السيح ورد كثيرين من اهلبا الى الايمان وعمدهم ثم بشر فى مدن! كثيرة مجاورة لها وبيدة عنها وكتب: له الرسول رسالتين ببثه فيهما على مداومة التعليم ويعرفه فيهما كيف يكون الاسقف والقس والشماس والارملة ويحذره من الرسل الكذيبة ويوصيه ان لا يضع يده على غير مستحق او قبل الامتحان وكان يدعو ابنه وحبيبه وارسل على يده ارعة رسائل ولما رعى رعية المسيح الذى اتمن عليه واضاء عليهم بتعليمه وتنبيهه وتجره" وردعه وكان
the apostle. This saint was born and raised in the city of Lystra (Lastrah). His father was Greek and worshipped idols; his mother was Jewish, living in the darkness of the Law. When the apostle preached the gospel at Lystra and this saint heard his teaching, then saw the signs that God most high performed through him—now intelligent men were surprised and philosophers were incapable of imitating them—this saint entered his group and made profession of believing in the Lord the Messiah at his hands. He was baptized in the name of the Holy Trinity, abandoned the gods of his father and their laws, and ceased to act according to the law of his mother. Then he became a disciple of the apostle, followed him on many journeys, and shared his fatigues. He experienced many pains and numerous sorrows. The apostle consecrated him bishop of the city of Ephesus (Efesos). When he entered it, he preached the gospel in the name of the Messiah, converted many of its inhabitants to the faith, and baptized them. Then he announced the gospel in many neighboring and distant cities. The apostle wrote him two epistles where he encourages him to continue his teaching and where he teaches him the duties of the bishop, the priest, the deacon, and the widows, and distrust against false prophets; he exhorts him not to impose hands on anyone who is not worthy or before an examination; he called him his son and his friend. He sent four epistles through his mediation. When Timothy guarded the flock of the Messiah that was entrusted to him, enlightened it by his teaching and his warnings, stimulated it, and turned it away, closing the mouths of the Jews and the Greeks, the crowd hated him, gathered


[p. 682] {#po.11.p682}
مداوما لتبكيت البهود والبونانبين فحسدوا الجميع واجتمعوا عليه بافس وقتلوه ودفن جسده قوم من المومنين با فى مثل هذا اليوم مم ان الملك القديس قسطلطين اهتم نقد جسده فنقله من افسس الى مدينة القسطنطينية وكان وصوله اليها فى البوم السايع والعشرين من طوبه وعيد له ايضا فى ذلك اليوم صلواته تكون معنا امين
against him at Ephesus, and killed him. A troop of the faithful buried his body there, on the anniversary of this day. Later, when Saint Constantine (Qostantin) reigned, he took care to have his body transported from Ephesus to Constantinople. It arrived there on the 27th of Toubeh, and his feast is celebrated there on this same day. May his prayers be with us! Amen.
اليوم الرابع والعشرون من طوة”
TWENTY-FOURTH * OF TOUBEH (January 19).
فى! هذا اليوم تتبحت القديسة الناسكة مريم هذد كانت ابنة ابوين مسيحيين من اشراف مدينة الاسكندرية فطلبها اولاد اكابر المديثة للزيجة فما سمحت ليم بذلك ولما تبحا والديها فرقت كلما كان خلفلا لها على الفقراء والمساكين واخذت هى من حملته شيا يرا ودخلت الى بعض ديارات القديسين العذارى بظاهر الاسكندرية ولست فيه الثوب المقدس واجهدت نفسبا فى عبادات كثيرة فمكثت خمسة عشر سنة مجاهدة من النوم الى ان غلبته فلم تنظر نائمة نهارا قط وكانت فى هذه المدة مداومة الصيام الى المسا كسنة ديرها وعند
On this day died the holy ascetic Mary (Maryam). She was the daughter of Christian parents from among the nobles of Alexandria (El-Iskandaryah). The sons of the city's leaders asked for her hand in marriage, but she refused. When her parents died, she distributed everything they left her to the poor and the unfortunate; she took a small amount and entered one of the holy convents of virgins outside Alexandria. She donned the sacred garment there and applied herself to numerous acts of worship. She remained there for fifteen years, struggling against sleep until she conquered it. She was never seen sleeping during the day. During this time, she fasted without interruption until evening, following the custom of her convent. Then she donned the holy habit


[p. 683] {#po.11.p683}
ذلك لبست الاسكيم المقدس وعند لباسه نزعت ما كان عليها من الثياب الصوف ولست محا من شن ثم سألت الرئيسة ان تسمح ان تحبس ذاتها لتجرب تفسها ان كانت تطيق ذلك فلما شارت عليها ان تحمس تفسبا فى قلابتها فدخات القلاية واغلقت بابها وفتحت طاق صغيرةٌ تتناول منها الحاحة وتتقرب منها فمكثت حبيسة فى القلاءة انين وعشرين سنة وكات فى طول هذه المدة قائمة فى الطول انهار لا تقعد على الارض بل مصلية دائما وفى الليل كانت تام بعضه ثم تصلى بقيته وكات تصوم يومين يومين ثم تاكل بعدهيا خزا يابس تنله بماء وفى ايام الارسين لا تذوق خزا بل تنال بعد ثلاثة ايام باقلا مبلول ولما دنا اليوم الحادى عشر من طوبه استدعت من الماه المقدس وفسات منه وجها وايدبها ثم تقريت وشريت من ذلك الماء المقدس ثم تمرضت ولزمت الفراش الى اليوم الحادى والعشرين من طوبه فتقريت ايضا فى ذلك اليوم واستدعت الرئيسة بسوأل كثير ان تاوليا رجليها فلما اجات سوألبا قلتهما ومرغت وحها عليهما وقالت الشكر لباتين القدمين فهما اللتان اوصلانى الى السيد المسيح ثم استدعت جماعة الاخوة فودعتهم ثم سأتهم ان يفتقدوها من بعد ثلانة ايام
When she put it on, she cast off the woolen garments she was wearing and covered herself with a hair shirt. Then she asked the superior to allow her to live as a recluse to test herself and see if she could endure it. When she received permission to lock herself in her cell, she entered it, closed the door, and opened a small window through which she received what was necessary for her and took communion. She remained a recluse in her cell for twenty-two years. During all this time, she did not sit on the ground during the day, but she prayed continually, slept a part of the night, and prayed during the rest. She fasted every two days, then she ate dry bread that she moistened with water. During the forty days (of Lent), she did not taste bread, but every three days she received vegetables in water. When the 21st of Toubeh approached, she asked for holy water with which she washed her face and hands; then she took communion and drank of this holy water. Then she fell ill and could no longer leave her bed until the 21st * of Toubeh; she took communion again that day and asked for the superior. She begged her insistently to give her her feet. When the latter had consented to her request, the saint kissed them and passed her face over them, saying: "Thanks to these two feet, for it is they that make me arrive until Our Lord the Messiah." Then she asked for all the sisters, bade them farewell, and asked them to come and visit her after three days. When the twenty-fourth


[p. 684] {#po.11.p684}
فلما كان اليوم الرابع والعشرين من طوبه افتقدوها فوجدوها قد تنيحت فحبلوها الى اليعة وصلوا عليها ثم وضعوها مع اجساد العذارى القديسات صلاتها! تكون معنا أمين تتيح انبا افراهام بجل فرجود المعروف بدير جدة وهذا القدين فمضى الى دير القديس ابو بخوم واقام عندهم اياما فلما رأود بنك عظيم البسود الاسكيم وبكث يزيد على عادته الى المكان الذى ظهز فيه بنكارس ووصات رسالة انا افراهام من مدينة القسطنطينية وهو عرف الرهبان بما جرى عليه من يوستيانوس الملك الخلقدونى لانه قال تعال تقرب معى وعود الى ديرك على رياستك كما كنت اولا وان خالفتتى فلا ترجع تعود الى الدير فاعتفى القديس انا افراهام من رياسته وترك الدير فارسل الملك المرذول بتكارس ومعه عسكر وامرلا ان من اطاعه على امانة الملك فيقى نات فى الدير يمن خالف ينفوه فخرجوا الرهبان وتفرقوا فى البرارى والاديرة فاما هذا القديس انا افراهام فمضى الى دير ابو شنودة بجيل ادرية وسكن فيه وبدأ ينقل قوانين للقديس ابو شنودة
of Toubeh arrived, they came to see her and found her dead. They carried her to the church, prayed over her, and then placed her with the bodies of the holy virgins. May her prayer be with us! Amen. Anbâ Ephraim (Afrähäm) died on the mountain of Fargout (Fardjoud), known by the name of Deir Djadah. This saint went to the convent of Saint Abou Bakhoum and remained there for several days. When he was seen to be such a great devotee, he was clothed in the habit, and he remained there redoubling his devotions until the arrival of Bankâres. The epistle of Anbâ Ephraim arrived from the city of Constantinople (El-Qostantinyah); he informed the monks of what had happened to him at the hands of Justin (Youstiânous) the emperor, a partisan of the Council of Chalcedon, for he had said to him: "Come, commune with me, then return to your convent according to your rank, as you were before. If you resist me, you will no longer return to your monastery." Anbâ Ephraim stripped himself of his dignity and abandoned the monastery. The despicable emperor sent Bankâres and with him his soldiers with this order: "Whoever obeys by recognizing the faith of the emperor, let him remain quiet in his convent; whoever refuses, drive him out." The monks went out and dispersed into the deserts and monasteries. As for Saint Anbâ Ephraim,


[p. 685] {#po.11.p685}
ويكتبها فى اوراق الى ان كملبا وجعليم فى امطان وختميم وارسليم الى دير القديس ابو مسيس واوصاهم ان يتحفظوا بهم وهو يقول فى رسالته اليهم ان فى هذا الامطار حبوب وزراريع منتجية فحفظوهم عندهم اياما وعد ذلك ان البقولات الذين كانوا فى دير القديس ابو مسيس فرغت ولم يجدوا حبوب يزرعوا ففكروا من اجل الامطار الذى كان القديس انا افراهام ارسليم الهم وداعة ففتحوا وهم يظنوا انهم زراديع فوجدوا فيهم القوانين الذى وضعبم ابونا اننا شنودة لان الرهبان الذين كانوا فى دير القديس ابو شنودة ما كانوا يمكنوا احدا من نقابم ولما وقفوا عليه رهيان ابو مسيس ففرحوا بهم ونقلوهم بسرعة وبعد ايام خرج القديس انا افراهام بوحى من الرب وخاء وسكن فى ديرا بحاجر فرجود ونا الدير واجتمعت اليه اناس كثير والبسهم الاسكيم واتوا اليه عذارى وطلبوا اليه ان يبنى ليم هنادة! ويمكثوا تحت صلاته فالسهم الاسكيم ونا ليم! دير وسكنوا فيه وانه طلب القوانين حتى يعلم اولادة الرهبان الرجال والنساء فمضى متحدرا الى دين ابو مسيس فوجديا الامطار
he went to the convent of Anbâ Chenouti (Chenoudah) on the mountain of Atripe (Adribah); he lived there and began to transcribe the regulations attributed to Saint Abou Chenouti and to write them on sheets of paper, until he had finished them. He placed them in vases that he sealed and sent to the convent of Saint Abou Masis, recommending that the monks keep them. In the letter he wrote to them, he said: "There are in these vases seeds and salutary grains." They kept them with them for a few days. Then, the vegetables that were in the convent of Saint Abou Masis being exhausted, as they could not find grains to sow, they thought of the vases that Saint Anbâ Ephraim had sent them in deposit; they opened them, believing that there were seeds, and found there the rules that our father Abou Chenouti had established, for none of the monks who were in that convent was capable of transcribing them. When the monks of Abou Masis had knowledge of it, they rejoiced and transcribed them quickly. A few days later, Saint Anbâ Ephraim went out by a divine revelation, came to live in his convent, on the rock of Fargout, and he built a monastery. Many people gathered around him: he clothed them in the habit. Virgins also came to find him and asked him to build them a refuge (?) to dwell there under the shelter of his prayer. He clothed them in the habit and built them a convent where they lived. He sought the regulations to instruct his children, monks and nuns, and he went down to the convent of Abou Masis. The vases were


[p. 686] {#po.11.p686}
مفتوحة وقد تقلوا القوانين الذين كانوا فيهم فعز عليه ذلك وخاصمبم قائلا ليم فلو كانوا هذا الامطار لغيرى ما كنتم تجسروا تفتحوهم فضرب له رئيس دير ابو مسيس المطانوة واستغفر منه وقال له هذا نور قد انبسط علينا كلنا فلا يعز عليك هذا فعاد الى دير ومعه القوانين وكان يقراها على اولاده الرجال والنساء وعلمهم ونسظهم! وفيما هو ذات ليلة قائم فى الصلاة ظهر له ملاك الرب وقال له قد دعاك الرب الى درجة القسيسية فقال له ياسيدى كيف اقدر على ذلك ولم احفظ شى من قوائين القداس فلقنه الملاك وامره بدابة قانون الاب ساوبرس البطريرك العظيم فقراه القديس للوقت واكمله ومضى الى عند الاسقف وكرزه قيس وقدس بذلك القانون وكان رحلا من اهل فرجود قال لزوحته هوذا اتى تعرفى يااختى ان هذا غلاء عظيم صعب منسط على الديار المصرية باسرها فاتفقى على رأى تصنعه فاخذوا القمح الذى ليم وسكبوه فى مطمورة وكان فها عشرة اراذب قمح ووضعوا علا قريانة وختموها وقال لبا امضى الى دير الرهبانات وانا امضى الى دير الرهبان عند الاب انا افراهام حتى يمضى ايام الفلاء فتعود الى بيتنا ونجد القمح موفر ونتيش به فصنعوا كذلك
found open and the regulations that were in them had been transcribed. This was painful to him, and he blamed the religious, saying: "If these vases had belonged to someone other than me, you would not have dared to open them." The superior of the convent of Abou Masis beat his breast, implored his pardon, and said to him: "It is a light that has spread over us all: may this not be painful to you." The saint returned to his convent, having with him the regulations that he read to his children, monks and nuns; he instructed and exhorted them. One night as he was standing in prayer, the angel of the Lord appeared to him and said: "The Lord calls you to the rank of the priesthood." - "Lord," he replied, "how can I, when I know nothing of the rules of the liturgy?" Then the angel instructed him and ordered him to begin the rule of our father the great patriarch Severus (Sâouiros). The saint recited it at once completely. He went to find the bishop who ordained him, and he officiated according to this rule. There was one of the inhabitants of Fargout who said to his wife: "You know, my sister, that there is a great and painful dearth spread over all the land of Egypt (Misr); let us both agree on a plan that we will execute." They took wheat that belonged to them and poured it into a silo; there were ten ardebs of it. They placed a host on top and sealed it. Then he said to her: "Go to the convent of the nuns, and I will go to that of the monks, near the father Anbâ Ephraim, until the days of the dearth arrive; then we will return to our house, we will find plenty of grain, and we will live on it." When


[p. 687] {#po.11.p687}
ولما اتقضى الغلاء ورخصت الاسعار نظر فى الحلم كان له انان يسلم له نصف قريانة التى كان وضعبا على باب المطمورة وانه استبقظ وهو مرجوف فوجد نصف القريانة يده على التحقيق بض للوقت ومضى الى دير الرهبانات واستدعى زوحته واعلمها بما قد نظر» فى الحلم واوراها نصف القريائة التى دفعوها فقالت له هكذا انا ايضا رأيت هذا الحلم بمينه واخرجت له نصف القربانة التى دفعوها لبا بالليل فتركها على النصف الاخر التى كان مع زوجا فاختم الكسر فلموا للوقت انها تلك القريانة التى وضعوها على فم المطمورة فمضوا للوقت الى يتهم وفتحوا المطمودة فلم يجدوا فيها شى ولا القريانة ايضا فتعجبوا من احكام الله العادلة التى لا يريد الظلم واخيروا عن هذا انا افراهام ان كان يجتمع اليه اناس كثير فى ذلك الغلاه الصعب وكان يقوم بهم وليس يرد احد فارغ وفى الاخير قل الخز فتقدم اليه صاحب الداقنية وعرفه فقال له اهتم اليوم الرب الاله ما ينسانا وانه مضى الى موضع الخبز فضم الفضلات واللباب فى زيل واتى به وطرحه قدام القديس بانزعاج وحنق فامر القديس ان يعملوا القريان وقدس الى وقت الانجيل وكان يطلب من الرب فسمعوا وجبة
the famine had arrived and food became expensive, he saw in a dream that a man was handing him half of the host that he had placed on the door of the silo. He awoke all trembling and indeed found half of the host in his hand. He rose at once, went to the convent of the nuns, asked for his wife, informed her of what he had seen in his dream, and showed her the half of the host that had been handed to him. - She said to him: "I also had the same dream," and produced for him the half of the host that had been handed to her that night. He applied it against the other half, and the two parts fit together entirely. They recognized at once that it was this host that they had placed on the opening of the silo. They went without delay to their home, opened the silo, and found nothing in it anymore but the host. They were astonished at the equitable judgments of God who does not love injustice, and they informed Anbâ Ephraim of it. While numerous people were gathering around him in this hard famine, he supported them and sent no one away empty-handed. Later, the bread ran out; the steward went to find the saint and informed him of it. The other said to him: "I will take care of it today, and the Lord God will not forget us." The other went to the place where the bread was kept, gathered the remains and crumbs in a basket, carried it, and threw it before the saint with violence and anger. Ephraim ordered the holy sacrifice to be celebrated; when they were at the Gospel, he invoked the Lord: a great noise was heard at that moment. The father turned to the steward and said to him: "Go out and see what this great noise is." He


[p. 688] {#po.11.p688}
عظيمة وقت الانجيل فالتفت الاب للرجل الذى على الدياقنية وقال اخرج وابصر هذه الوحية العظيمة فخرج للوقت ومعه الاخونة ومضوا الى الدياقنية وفتحوا الباب فخرج الخز الى برا ولم يقدروا يخلقوا الباب فلما فرغوا القداس ابصرهم الاب ان يعمروا الموائد للناس الحاضرين من بركة الله ولم يزالوا ياكلوا من الخيز الذى خرج من الاب الى فروغ الغلاء قبل ان يقدروا يغلقوا! الباب ولما رحم الله شعبه وطلع اليل فزرعوا فاكله الدود فاتوا الى الاب وشكوا له حال الدود فصلى وطلب من الرب ففنظر عمود ثار امامه واتاد صوتا قائلا ياافراهام قول لهم يصنعوا هكذا يخلصوا يقدوا النار في وسط كل حقل فمضوا ووقدوا النار فى المحاقل3 فكان الدود يجتمع كله الى النار وحترق وزرعوا الناس يفرح عظيم وسلموا من الدود بصلاة هذا القديس انا افراهام لان يعقوب يقول ان صلاة البار قونة جدا وتفعل4 ثم ان هذا القديس تزيد فى العبادة وبخرج الى السياحة مرار كثيرة وعود الى ديرة ويجاهد فى جنده الليل ولنهار وتنيح ونضى الى السيد السيح بشيخوخة حسنة وهو قائل ها انا واللنون الذى اعطيتنى الرب يرحمنا بصلاته أمين
went out at once with all the brothers; they went to the office, opened the door: the bread came out to the outside without one being able to close it. When the holy sacrifice was finished, the saint ordered them to cover the tables for the people present; by the blessing of the Lord, they did not cease to eat bread that came out of the door, without being able to close it until the end of the dearth. When the Lord had pity on his flock and the Nile rose, they sowed, but the worms ate the seed. They went to find the father and complained about it. He prayed and implored the Lord: he saw before him a pillar of fire, and a voice said to him: "Ephraim, tell them to act thus: let them light a fire in the middle of each field." They went to light a fire in the fields: all the worms gathered there and burned themselves. The people sowed with great joy and were delivered from the worms by the prayer of this saint Anbâ Ephraim, for James (Ya'qoub) says: "The prayer of the virtuous man is very powerful and acts." Then the saint redoubled his devotions: he went frequently on journeys, returned to his convent, and tormented his body night and day. He died and went to find Our Lord the Messiah with a beautiful old age, saying: "Here I am with the children that you have given me." May the Lord have mercy on us by his prayer! Amen.


[p. 689] {#po.11.p689}
وفى! هذا اليوم ايضا استشهد انا ابساده وهو من قرية من اعمال مدينة الهنسا وابولا من القيس وامه من اهريت وكانت من حجنس كبنة الاصنام ثم انها امنت بالمسيح وان كاهن عبد الاوثان طلبها يتزوحها فهيت الى القيس فسمع با انسان بخولى مزارع فطلبها وازوحها لانه كان مسيحى وانهم رزقوا هذا القديس ففرحوا به فرح كثير وعملوا وليمة لاهل بلدهم وجمعوا المساكين والضعف لما كبر الصبى بدأ يتعلم خوف الله وحمل بوصايا الانجيل حتى اوفى عشرين سنة تنيح والده وخلف اموال كثيرةٍ وانه بقى يزداد في عمل البر والصدقات والرحمة الى الزمان الذى وصل فيه مرسوم ديقلاديانوين الملك الكافر بحث النان على عادة الاوثان فاحتبس فى يتنه وبدأ يصلى وطلب من الله ولما كان ذات ليلة وهو يصِلى اشرق عليه نور عقليم وظهر له اليد المخلص واعطاد السلام وقال .له لماذا ات متوانى والجباد مبسوط قم باكرا وامضى الى مدينة القيس وانت تجد يوتقنا كبير المدينة
On this day Anbâ Ibchâdeh (Absâdah) suffered martyrdom. He was from a city in the government of Behnesâ; his father was from Kaïs (El-Qis) and his mother from Ehrit, from a family of priests of the idols: then she believed in the Messiah. - A priest, a worshipper of idols, asked for her hand in marriage: she fled to Kaïs. A guardian of the harvests heard about it; he asked for her and married her, for he was a Christian. They were gratified with this saint as a son and felt great joy: they made a feast for the people of their city and gathered the poor and the unfortunate. When the young man was grown, he began to learn the fear of God and to practice the precepts of the Gospel until he reached his twenty years. His father died, leaving him considerable wealth. He remained redoubling good deeds, alms, and compassion until the time when the order of Diocletian (Diglâdyänous), the infidel emperor, arrived, pushing people to worship idols. He locked himself in his home and began to pray and to implore God. One night, while he was praying, a great light shone: Our Lord the Savior appeared to him, gave him salvation, and said to him: "Why are you soft while the struggle is spreading? Rise early and go to the city of Kaïs; you will find Youtganâ, the chief of the city; they have given him this charge so that he may persecute the Christians; suffer a short torture and you will obtain the crown of


[p. 690] {#po.11.p690}
وقد ولوه يعذب المسيحيين فاحتمل عذاب يسير تال أكليل المجد وعرفه انك تقف فى ثلثة مجالس حكم واخبرا بكلما يجرى عليه واعطاه السلام وصعد الى السماء ولما كان من الغد لم يتوانى بل انه مضى كما امره السيد المخلص فوجد الحاكم جالسا وهم يقدموا له النصارى فتقدم بسرعة وصرخ قائلا انا نصرانى فقدموا للوقت وامر ان يرفع! من على الارض وضرب بالسياط والدبايس على رأسه وأمر ان تقلع اظفارة وضمسوا فى الخل والجير فتقدم والى الليل وشفع فى القديس قائلا يامولاى لا تعجل بالعجلة بالقتل لان على هذا خراج كثير بل امهله ولعله يرفع البخور للوقت ارسله الى السجن وكان هناك رجل قد بلع تعبان صغير وكبر فى بطنه وقارب الموت فصلى القديس عليه وطلع من فاه وشفى الرجل..وامن بالسيد المسيح فظبر خض القديس فى المدينة فاحضروا اليه احد الاراخنة اسمه اوديمون ونه مرض الاستسقاء فدهنه الشهيد بالزيت وصلى عليه فرى من ساعته ومجد الله لان كلمن كان به اصناف الامراض فيصلى عليهم فيوهبهم الرب الشفاء وبامنوا بالسيح ثم ان والدنه جاءت اليه وهى باكية صارخة وكلمته وهى قائلة له ياولدى الحبيب ارفع
glory; teach him that you will be in three sessions of justice and inform him of everything that will happen to him." He gave him salvation and ascended back to heaven. From the next day, Ibchadeh did not delay: he left as Our Lord the Savior had ordered him. He found the magistrate sitting; they were presenting the Christians to him. The saint advanced in all haste and cried: "I am a Christian." They took him at once, and the judge ordered him to be raised above the ground, to be struck on the head with whips and clubs, to have his nails torn out, and to be plunged into vinegar and lime. Then the governor of El-Lil (?) had pity on the saint, advanced, and said: "Master, do not be in a hurry to make him perish, for he pays many taxes; give him a respite, and perhaps he will offer incense." At once, he sent him to prison. There was a man who had swallowed a small dragon: it had grown in his belly, and the man was near to dying: the saint prayed over him, the dragon came out through his mouth, and the sick man was healed and believed in Our Lord the Messiah. The reputation of the saint was known in the city: they brought to him a functionary named Eudemon (Audaïmoun) who was suffering from dropsy. The martyr anointed him with oil and prayed over him: he was healed at once and praised God: in fact, all those who were afflicted with various diseases, over whom he prayed and whom God healed, believed in the Messiah. Then his mother came to find him, weeping and crying, and spoke to him thus: "My


[p. 691] {#po.11.p691}
البخور للاصنام واذا مضيت توب عن ما عملته فغضب علا وقال ليا امضى صومى وصلى حتى يففر لك الرب ما قد قلتيه فاجابته قائلة ياولدى انا ما هرت من بلدى الا لاجل المسيح حتى ييقى علتى اسمه وودعته وذهبت الى بيتها واظبرت تونة عليمة واما عم الشبيد فكان يحسده لاحل الاموال الذى له وانه مضى الى الامير وعرفه ان ابساده فى السجن ياتون اليه اهل المدينة بالمرض وشفيهم وامنوا بالمسيح فاحضر وقال له ارفع البخور لمعبودات الملك فلم يقدر عليه فادسله الى وإلى البنساء وهو مربوط فى ذنب حصان قلما وصلوا الاجناد فطلبوا الوالى فلم يجدود لانه كان مضى الى الفيوم فذهيوا ولما وصلوا الى باب مدينة الفيوم وجدوا مقدم المدينة ينى فى دار وكان له ولد وحيد قائما إيستحث العمالين قط حجر كبير من احد العمالين على رأس الصبى فانضف مخه ومات لوقته فاسكوا النايين يريد يقتلوهم وكات ضحة عظيمة فلما ابص الشهيد ما كان قال للاجناد احلونى! من الرباط وامر الجموع ان يبعدوا عن الميت قليل وسط يديه وصلى وطلاب من الرب بض الميت لوقته وسلموا لابود واطلقوا البنابين وفرحوا الجموع وقالوا
dear son, offer incense to the idols, and when you have left, repent of what you have done." He became angry with her and said to her: "Go away, fast and pray, so that God may forgive you for what you have said." She answered him: "My son, I only fled from my city because of the Messiah, so that his name might always be upon me." She bade him farewell, left for her house, and manifested a lively repentance. As for the uncle of the martyr, he was jealous of him because of his wealth. He went to find the governor and informed him that, while Ibchâdeh was in prison, the people of the city were bringing him the sick whom he healed, and they were believing in the Messiah. He had him brought and said to him: "Offer incense to the divinities of the emperor." He could obtain nothing from him. Then he sent him to the governor of Behnesâ, tied to the tail of a stallion. When the guards arrived and asked for the governor, they did not find him, for he had gone to Phiom (El-Fayoum).
